**“A Book Of Life To Rebuild The World.”**

Below is a complete, rigorously planned blueprint with **all phases fully specified**, including objectives, tasks, deliverables, dependencies, success criteria, and timelines.

---

## 1. Vision & Core Principles

**Mission:** Create a living, decentralized, offline‑first knowledge engine that enables any community to rebuild civilization from first principles—covering survival, agriculture, fabrication, energy, health, governance, and culture.

**Core Principles:**
- **Offline‑First & Field‑Ready:** Every feature works with zero internet.
- **Forkable & Decentralized:** The entire knowledge base is a portable directory; anyone can mirror or diverge.
- **Agent‑Readable & Human‑Readable:** Structured for both AI retrieval and human browsing.
- **Verifiable & Sourced:** Every claim links to public‑domain or open‑license sources with provenance tracking.
- **Actionable over Academic:** Step‑by‑step procedures, schematics, and field‑tested methods take priority.

---

## 2. Content Architecture

### 2.1 Eight Domains (Expanded from Seed Dataset)

The seed dataset contains 23 chapters. These are reorganized into **8 Domains**, each with **Cross‑Cutting Threads**.

| Domain | Seed Chapters Mapped | New Additions |
|---|---|---|
| **Survival & Immediate Needs** | First Things, Food (partial), Medicine (partial) | Water purification at scale, NBC preparedness, psychological first aid, emergency shelter, rescue signaling |
| **Agriculture & Food Systems** | Plants, Animals, Food | Permaculture, seed saving, aquaponics, soil regeneration, food forests, livestock breeding, preservation |
| **Materials & Fabrication** | Materials, Tools, Mechanics | Foundry work, blacksmithing, machining, 3D printing, glassblowing, ceramics, papermaking, adhesives |
| **Energy & Power** | Electricity, Industry (partial) | Solar PV fabrication, wind turbines, micro‑hydro, biogas, wood gasification, battery chemistry, power electronics |
| **Construction & Infrastructure** | Building, Mechanics (partial) | Timber framing, masonry, water wells, sanitation, roads, bridges, dams, surveying |
| **Health & Medicine** | Medicine | Anatomy, diagnostics without labs, field surgery, antibiotics, vaccines, dental care, mental health, childbirth |
| **Knowledge & Communication** | Word, Art, Music, Optics | Printing, radio, cryptography, library organization, oral history, education, art & music preservation |
| **Society & Governance** | Society, Human, Games | Conflict resolution, consensus decision‑making, legal frameworks, economics, community organizing, ethics |

### 2.2 Cross‑Cutting Metadata (Every Entry)

- **Difficulty:** Novice / Apprentice / Journeyman / Master
- **Prerequisites:** Linked skill or material entries
- **Time to Complete:** Hours / Days / Weeks
- **Materials Required:** Linked to material entries
- **Safety Level:** Green / Yellow / Red
- **Source Provenance:** Public domain, CC‑BY‑SA, original, field‑tested
- **Verification Status:** Untested / Peer‑Reviewed / Field‑Tested (date & location)
- **Geographic Applicability:** Temperate / Arid / Tropical / Arctic / Universal

---

## 3. Technical Architecture (Production‑Grade)

### 3.1 Repository Structure (File‑over‑App)

```
book-of-life/
├── README.md
├── LICENSE (CC-BY-SA 4.0 + MIT for code)
├── CONTRIBUTING.md
├── CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md
├── manifest.toml
├── domains/               # Source of truth (Markdown + assets)
│   ├── survival/
│   ├── agriculture/
│   ├── materials/
│   ├── energy/
│   ├── construction/
│   ├── health/
│   ├── knowledge/
│   └── society/
├── sidecars/              # Metadata sidecars (.pnk.yaml) per document
├── scripts/               # Build, index, validation
├── build/                 # Generated artifacts (gitignored)
│   ├── index.db
│   ├── offline-bundle/
│   └── api/
├── deploy/
│   ├── docker-compose.yml
│   ├── raspberry-pi/
│   └── kiwix/
└── .github/               # CI/CD workflows
```

### 3.2 Content Format: Markdown + Sidecar YAML

Each entry is a Markdown file with structured frontmatter plus a sidecar YAML for stable IDs, tags, links, and provenance.

**Example sidecar (`water-purification.pnk.yaml`):**
```yaml
id: pnk:01H8X.../survival/water-purification
tags: [water, sanitation, survival, chemistry]
difficulty: novice
prerequisites: []
safety: green
sources:
  - "WHO Guidelines for Drinking-water Quality (public domain)"
  - "US Army FM 21-10 Field Sanitation (public domain)"
verification:
  status: field-tested
  tested_by: "Community Team Alpha"
  date: "2026-03-15"
  location: "Arizona, USA"
links:
  - pnk:01H8X.../survival/fire-making
  - pnk:01H8X.../materials/charcoal-production
```

### 3.3 Search & Retrieval: Hybrid BM25 + Vector

- **SQLite FTS5** for BM25 keyword search.
- **Local embeddings** (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`) for semantic search.
- **Reciprocal Rank Fusion** to combine results.
- Entirely offline, CPU‑friendly.

**Indexing Pipeline:**
1. Parse Markdown + sidecar YAML.
2. Chunk by heading with overlap.
3. Generate embeddings locally.
4. Store in SQLite (FTS5 + vector index).
5. Build `manifest.json` for offline clients.

### 3.4 API Layer: MCP (Model Context Protocol)

Expose the KB via an MCP server so AI agents can query it.

Tools:
- `bookofLife_search(query, domain?, difficulty?)`
- `bookofLife_get(entry_id)`
- `bookofLife_linked(entry_id)`
- `bookofLife_verify(entry_id)`

### 3.5 Offline Deployment: Kiwix + Docker Bundle

- **Kiwix ZIM** for offline browsing on any device.
- **Docker Compose** bundle with Kiwix, Ollama (local LLM), offline maps (OpenStreetMap), and Kolibri (education).
- Runs on a single Linux PC with 4GB RAM; scales to GPU.

### 3.6 Engineering Practices

| Concern | Implementation |
|---|---|
| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions: lint Markdown, validate sidecars, build index, link‑check, generate ZIM, publish releases |
| **Testing** | Unit tests for parsers; integration tests for search; smoke tests for Docker |
| **Versioning** | Semantic versioning per entry; global KB version in `manifest.toml` |
| **Licensing** | CC‑BY‑SA 4.0 for content; MIT for code; per‑file headers |
| **Security** | No executable code in content; signed releases; SBOM for Docker images |
| **Accessibility** | Plain‑text fallbacks; screen‑reader‑friendly HTML |
| **Observability** | Opt‑in query logging; index freshness metrics |

---

## 4. Governance & Community Model

### 4.1 Contribution Tiers

| Tier | Role | Permissions |
|---|---|---|
| **Reader** | Browse, search, download | Public |
| **Contributor** | Submit PRs for content, translations | Signed CLA |
| **Verifier** | Field‑test entries, mark verification status | Nominated by maintainers |
| **Domain Maintainer** | Review PRs for a domain, resolve conflicts | Elected per domain |
| **Core Maintainer** | Release management, architecture, governance | Consensus of maintainers |

### 4.2 Verification Workflow

Every entry follows a lifecycle:
1. **Draft** (unverified)
2. **Peer‑Reviewed** (two contributors review for accuracy)
3. **Field‑Tested** (at least one verifier reproduces the procedure and documents results)
4. **Deprecated** (superseded by better method)

Field‑test reports stored alongside the entry (photos, measurements, failure modes).

### 4.3 Decentralized Mirroring

Encourage forks and mirrors. Links between KBs work via `pnk://` URIs that survive renames and moves. Prevents single point of failure.

---

## 5. Integration with Existing Open‑Source Ecosystems

- **Appropedia:** Appropriate technology knowledge (CC‑BY‑SA).
- **Open Source Ecology (GVCS):** Blueprints for 50 industrial machines.
- **Kiwix:** ZIM packaging for offline distribution.
- **Project NOMAD:** Docker stack for offline AI + Wikipedia + maps.
- **Axiom Recovery Library:** Raspberry Pi image recipes for field deployment.
- **Internet Archive & Project Gutenberg:** Public‑domain books as appendices.
- **DPLA:** Metadata standards for cultural heritage items.

---

## 6. Expansion Beyond Seed Data: Missing Critical Domains

The seed dataset covers 23 chapters. To be truly civilization‑rebuilding, the following must be added:

- **Water Systems:** Wells, pumps, irrigation, treatment.
- **Sanitation & Hygiene:** Composting toilets, greywater, waste management, pest control.
- **Energy Storage:** Battery construction, pumped hydro, flywheel, thermal storage.
- **Chemistry & Biochemistry:** Soap, acids/bases, fermentation, essential oils.
- **Metallurgy:** Ore identification, smelting, alloying, heat treatment, casting.
- **Textiles:** Fiber processing, spinning, weaving, dyeing, sewing, leatherworking.
- **Transportation:** Cart & wagon building, boat building, animal harnessing.
- **Communication:** Radio transceivers, antennas, Morse code, signal mirrors.
- **Education:** Curriculum design, literacy, numeracy, apprenticeship models.
- **Governance & Law:** Constitution drafting, dispute resolution, property rights.
- **Economics:** Barter, local currency, accounting, supply chain resilience.
- **Culture & Meaning:** Art therapy, music therapy, ritual design, grief processing.
- **Ethics & Philosophy:** Triage ethics, intergenerational justice, environmental stewardship.

**Media Enrichment:**
- 3D models (OpenSCAD/FreeCAD)
- Video tutorials (offline MP4 with transcripts)
- Audio guides
- Interactive simulators (offline‑capable)
- Decision trees for triage and troubleshooting

**Localization & Cultural Adaptation:**
- i18n‑ready structure with per‑language sidecars
- Regional variants (tropical, arid, temperate, arctic)
- Indigenous knowledge (with permission and attribution)

## 7. Phased Development Plan (100% Detailed)

### Phase 0: Foundation & Scaffolding (Months 1–2)

**Objectives:** Establish repository, schemas, and CI/CD pipeline.

**Tasks:**
1. Create GitHub organization and repository.
2. Define directory structure (`domains/`, `sidecars/`, `scripts/`, `build/`, `deploy/`).
3. Write `manifest.toml` (KB version, sources, chunking config).
4. Design sidecar YAML schema (ID, tags, difficulty, prerequisites, safety, sources, verification, links).
5. Write validator script for sidecars (Python or Rust).
6. Set up GitHub Actions:
   - Markdown linting.
   - Sidecar validation.
   - Link checker.
   - Basic index build.
7. Write `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `LICENSE` (CC‑BY‑SA 4.0 + MIT).
8. Create issue templates and PR templates.
9. Recruit initial core maintainers (3–5 people).

**Deliverables:**
- Public repository with directory skeleton.
- Validator and CI passing on empty content.
- Governance documents.

**Dependencies:** None.

**Success Criteria:** Repository is publicly accessible, CI runs on every PR, and schema is documented.

---

### Phase 1: Seed Content Migration & Core Infrastructure (Months 3–5)

**Objectives:** Migrate all seed data into structured Markdown + sidecars; build indexing pipeline.

**Tasks:**
1. Extract all 23 chapters from seed dataset into Markdown files.
2. Organize into the 8 domains (some chapters split across domains).
3. Write sidecar YAML for each entry (ID, tags, difficulty, etc.).
4. Assign initial source provenance (public domain, original, etc.).
5. Build indexing pipeline:
   - Parser for Markdown + sidecar.
   - Chunker (by heading, 512‑token overlap).
   - Local embedding generation (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`).
   - SQLite FTS5 + vector index (sqlite‑vec or HNSW).
6. Write unit tests for parser and indexer.
7. Create initial API endpoints (static JSON export).
8. Publish first internal release (`v0.1.0-alpha`).

**Deliverables:**
- All seed content migrated (approx. 200–300 entries).
- Working search index (BM25 + vector).
- Static JSON API.
- Test suite.

**Dependencies:** Phase 0.

**Success Criteria:** Search returns relevant results for all seed topics; CI validates all sidecars.

---

### Phase 2: Search, API, and Offline Bundle (Months 6–8)

**Objectives:** Production‑grade search, MCP server, and offline deployment bundles.

**Tasks:**
1. Implement MCP server with tools:
   - `search`, `get`, `linked`, `verify`.
2. Integrate reciprocal rank fusion for hybrid search.
3. Build Kiwix ZIM file from generated HTML.
4. Create Docker Compose bundle:
   - Kiwix server.
   - Ollama (local LLM) for RAG.
   - Offline maps (OpenStreetMap).
   - Kolibri (education).
5. Write Raspberry Pi deployment recipe.
6. Add smoke tests for Docker bundle.
7. Publish `v0.2.0-beta` with offline bundle.

**Deliverables:**
- MCP server (documented, tested).
- Kiwix ZIM file.
- Docker Compose stack.
- Raspberry Pi image.

**Dependencies:** Phase 1.

**Success Criteria:** Docker bundle runs on 4GB RAM; MCP server responds to queries; ZIM file browsable offline.

---

### Phase 3: Verification & Community Governance (Months 9–12)

**Objectives:** Launch verification program and community governance.

**Tasks:**
1. Recruit domain maintainers (one per domain).
2. Establish verification workflow:
   - Draft → Peer‑Reviewed → Field‑Tested → Deprecated.
3. Create field‑test report template (photos, measurements, failure modes).
4. Launch call for verifiers.
5. Field‑test at least 50 entries across domains.
6. Implement verification badges in UI/API.
7. Hold first community governance meeting.
8. Publish `v0.3.0` with verification metadata.

**Deliverables:**
- 50+ field‑tested entries.
- Verification badges visible in search/API.
- Governance charter ratified.

**Dependencies:** Phase 2.

**Success Criteria:** At least 50 entries marked field‑tested; domain maintainers active; governance model documented and followed.

---

### Phase 4: Expansion & Enrichment (Months 13–18)

**Objectives:** Add missing domains, media, and localization.

**Tasks:**
1. Add new content for missing domains:
   - Water systems, sanitation, energy storage, chemistry, metallurgy, textiles, transportation, communication, education, governance, economics, culture, ethics.
2. Create 3D models (OpenSCAD/FreeCAD) for tools and machines.
3. Produce video tutorials (offline MP4 + transcripts).
4. Record audio guides.
5. Build interactive simulators (offline‑capable web apps).
6. Implement i18n framework; translate to 5 languages (Spanish, French, Hindi, Arabic, Swahili).
7. Add regional variants for tropical, arid, temperate, arctic.
8. Publish `v0.4.0` with 2,000+ entries.

**Deliverables:**
- 2,000+ entries across all domains.
- 3D model library.
- Video and audio library.
- 5 language translations.
- Regional variants.

**Dependencies:** Phase 3.

**Success Criteria:** All missing domains covered; media files playable offline; translations validated by native speakers.

---

### Phase 5: Ecosystem & Federation (Months 19–24)

**Objectives:** Open API for third‑party apps, federated KB network.

**Tasks:**
1. Publish public API specification (OpenAPI).
2. Create SDKs for Python, JavaScript, Rust.
3. Implement federation protocol:
   - `pnk://` URIs for cross‑KB linking.
   - Sync mechanism for mirrors.
4. Build mobile app (Android/iOS) with offline sync.
5. Establish annual “State of the Book” report.
6. Launch bug bounty and security audit.
7. Publish `v1.0.0` stable release.

**Deliverables:**
- Public API and SDKs.
- Federation protocol specification.
- Mobile app (beta).
- Annual report template.

**Dependencies:** Phase 4.

**Success Criteria:** Third‑party apps can query KB; mirrors sync; mobile app works offline.

---

### Phase 6: Sustainability & Long‑Term Maintenance (Months 25+)

**Objectives:** Ensure project survives and thrives.

**Tasks:**
1. Establish foundation or fiscal sponsor (e.g., Open Collective, Software Freedom Conservancy).
2. Create maintenance rotation for core maintainers.
3. Automate dependency updates and security patches.
4. Set up long‑term archival (Internet Archive, Zenodo).
5. Run annual community survey and roadmap review.
6. Expand to new domains as needed.

**Deliverables:**
- Sustainable funding model.
- Maintenance handbook.
- Archival copies.
- Annual roadmap.

**Dependencies:** Phase 5.

**Success Criteria:** Project has active maintainers, funding, and a growing user base; releases continue without interruption.

## 8. Risk Management

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Content accuracy | Rigorous verification workflow; field‑testing |
| Low contributor engagement | Clear onboarding; recognition; governance voice |
| Technical debt | Regular refactoring; automated tests; code reviews |
| Funding shortfall | Diversify funding (grants, donations, sponsorships) |
| Legal issues | Clear licensing; provenance tracking; legal review |
| Offline deployment complexity | Pre‑built images; documentation; community support |

# Book of Life – Complete Code Implementation

## Phase 0: Foundation & Scaffolding

### 0.1 Repository Root Files

**`README.md`**
```markdown
# Book of Life – Rebuild the World

> A living, decentralized, offline-first knowledge engine for rebuilding civilization from first principles.

[![CI](https://github.com/book-of-life/book-of-life/actions/workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/book-of-life/book-of-life/actions/workflows/ci.yml)
[![License: CC BY-SA 4.0](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-CC%20BY--SA%204.0-lightgrey.svg)](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/)
[![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-yellow.svg)](https://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)

## What is this?

**Book of Life** is an open-source, forkable, field-ready knowledge base covering every domain required to rebuild civilization: survival, agriculture, materials, energy, construction, health, knowledge, and society.

Every entry is:
- **Offline-first** — works with zero internet connectivity
- **Verifiable** — sourced and field-tested with provenance tracking
- **Actionable** — step-by-step procedures, schematics, and measurements
- **Agent-readable** — structured for both humans and AI retrieval

## Quick Start

```bash
# Clone the repository
git clone https://github.com/book-of-life/book-of-life.git
cd book-of-life

# Install dependencies
pip install -r requirements.txt

# Validate all sidecars
python scripts/validate.py

# Build the search index
python scripts/build_index.py

# Run the local server
python scripts/serve.py
```

## Offline Deployment

```bash
# Full offline bundle (Kiwix + Ollama + maps + Kolibri)
cd deploy/docker
docker compose up -d
```

## Repository Structure

```
book-of-life/
├── domains/          # Source of truth (Markdown + assets)
├── sidecars/         # Metadata sidecars (.pnk.yaml)
├── scripts/          # Build, index, validation
├── build/            # Generated artifacts (gitignored)
├── deploy/           # Deployment recipes
└── .github/          # CI/CD workflows
```

**`CONTRIBUTING.md`**
```markdown
# Contributing to Book of Life

Thank you for helping rebuild civilization. This document explains how to contribute.

## Contribution Tiers

| Tier | Role | Permissions |
|------|------|-------------|
| Reader | Browse, search, download | Public |
| Contributor | Submit PRs for content, translations | Signed CLA |
| Verifier | Field-test entries, mark verification status | Nominated by maintainers |
| Domain Maintainer | Review PRs for a domain | Elected per domain |
| Core Maintainer | Release management, architecture | Consensus of maintainers |

## Getting Started

1. Fork the repository.
2. Create a branch: `git checkout -b feat/your-topic`.
3. Add or edit content in `domains/<domain>/`.
4. Add or update the sidecar in `sidecars/<domain>/`.
5. Run validation: `python scripts/validate.py`.
6. Run the index build: `python scripts/build_index.py`.
7. Submit a pull request.

## Content Guidelines

- Every entry MUST have a sidecar YAML.
- Every claim MUST have a source (public domain, CC-BY-SA, or original).
- Procedures MUST be step-by-step and reproducible.
- Safety hazards MUST be marked `safety: red` or `safety: yellow`.
- Use inclusive, jargon-free language.

## Sidecar Schema

See `schemas/sidecar.schema.json` for the full schema.

## Verification Lifecycle

1. **Draft** — unverified
2. **Peer-Reviewed** — two contributors review for accuracy
3. **Field-Tested** — at least one verifier reproduces the procedure and documents results
4. **Deprecated** — superseded by a better method

## Code of Conduct

All contributors must follow our [Code of Conduct](CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md).

## License

By contributing, you agree your content is licensed under CC BY-SA 4.0 and
your code under MIT.
```

**`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`**
```markdown
# Contributor Code of Conduct

## Our Pledge

We as members, contributors, and leaders pledge to make participation in our
community a harassment-free experience for everyone, regardless of age, body
size, visible or invisible disability, ethnicity, sex characteristics, gender
identity and expression, level of experience, education, socio-economic status,
nationality, personal appearance, race, caste, color, religion, or sexual
identity and orientation.

## Our Standards

Examples of behavior that contributes to a positive environment:

- Demonstrating empathy and kindness toward other people
- Being respectful of differing opinions, viewpoints, and experiences
- Giving and gracefully accepting constructive feedback
- Accepting responsibility and apologizing to those affected by our mistakes
- Focusing on what is best for the overall community

Examples of unacceptable behavior:

- The use of sexualized language or imagery
- Trolling, insulting or derogatory comments, and personal or political attacks
- Public or private harassment
- Publishing others' private information without explicit permission
- Other conduct which could reasonably be considered inappropriate

## Enforcement

Instances of abusive, harassing, or otherwise unacceptable behavior may be
reported to the community leaders responsible for enforcement at
conduct@book-of-life.org. All complaints will be reviewed and investigated
promptly and fairly.

## Attribution

This Code of Conduct is adapted from the Contributor Covenant, version 2.1.
```

---

### 0.2 Manifest & Schemas

**`manifest.toml`**
```toml
[project]
name = "book-of-life"
version = "0.1.0-alpha"
description = "A living, decentralized, offline-first knowledge engine for rebuilding civilization."
license_content = "CC-BY-SA-4.0"
license_code = "MIT"
homepage = "https://book-of-life.org"
repository = "https://github.com/book-of-life/book-of-life"

[knowledge_base]
schema_version = "1.0.0"
default_language = "en"
supported_languages = ["en", "es", "fr", "de", "hi", "ar", "sw", "zh", "ja"]

[domains]
list = [
  "survival",
  "agriculture",
  "materials",
  "energy",
  "construction",
  "health",
  "knowledge",
  "society",
]

[chunking]
strategy = "heading"
max_tokens = 512
overlap_tokens = 64

[embeddings]
model = "all-MiniLM-L6-v2"
dimensions = 384
provider = "local"
backend = "onnx"

[index]
fts_engine = "sqlite-fts5"
vector_engine = "sqlite-vec"
fusion = "reciprocal_rank_fusion"
rrf_k = 60

[search]
default_limit = 20
max_limit = 100
min_score = 0.0

[api]
mcp_enabled = true
static_json_enabled = true
openapi_enabled = true

[build]
output_dir = "build"
index_path = "build/index.db"
offline_bundle_path = "build/offline-bundle"
api_path = "build/api"

[verification]
required_reviewers = 2
field_test_required = false
lifecycle = ["draft", "peer-reviewed", "field-tested", "deprecated"]

[safety]
levels = ["green", "yellow", "red"]

[difficulty]
levels = ["novice", "apprentice", "journeyman", "master"]
```

---

**`schemas/sidecar.schema.json`**
```json
{
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
  "$id": "https://book-of-life.org/schemas/sidecar.schema.json",
  "title": "Book of Life Sidecar",
  "description": "Metadata sidecar for a single knowledge entry.",
  "type": "object",
  "required": ["id", "title", "domain", "tags", "difficulty", "safety", "sources", "verification"],
  "additionalProperties": false,
  "properties": {
    "id": {
      "type": "string",
      "pattern": "^pnk:[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{26}/[a-z0-9-]+/[a-z0-9-]+$",
      "description": "Stable, unique identifier (ULID-based)."
    },
    "title": {
      "type": "string",
      "minLength": 3,
      "maxLength": 200
    },
    "domain": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": ["survival", "agriculture", "materials", "energy", "construction", "health", "knowledge", "society"]
    },
    "tags": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[a-z0-9-]+$" },
      "minItems": 1,
      "uniqueItems": true
    },
    "difficulty": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": ["novice", "apprentice", "journeyman", "master"]
    },
    "safety": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": ["green", "yellow", "red"]
    },
    "time_to_complete": {
      "type": "string",
      "enum": ["minutes", "hours", "days", "weeks", "months"]
    },
    "prerequisites": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^pnk:" },
      "default": []
    },
    "materials_required": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^pnk:" },
      "default": []
    },
    "tools_required": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^pnk:" },
      "default": []
    },
    "sources": {
      "type": "array",
      "minItems": 1,
      "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["title", "license"],
        "properties": {
          "title": { "type": "string" },
          "author": { "type": "string" },
          "url": { "type": "string", "format": "uri" },
          "license": {
            "type": "string",
            "enum": ["public-domain", "CC-BY-4.0", "CC-BY-SA-4.0", "CC0-1.0", "MIT", "original", "field-tested"]
          },
          "year": { "type": "integer" }
        }
      }
    },
    "verification": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["status"],
      "properties": {
        "status": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": ["draft", "peer-reviewed", "field-tested", "deprecated"]
        },
        "reviewers": {
          "type": "array",
          "items": { "type": "string" }
        },
        "tested_by": { "type": "string" },
        "date": { "type": "string", "format": "date" },
        "location": { "type": "string" },
        "notes": { "type": "string" }
      }
    },
    "links": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^pnk:" },
      "default": []
    },
    "geographic_applicability": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "string",
        "enum": ["temperate", "arid", "tropical", "arctic", "universal"]
      },
      "default": ["universal"]
    },
    "language": {
      "type": "string",
      "pattern": "^[a-z]{2}(-[A-Z]{2})?$",
      "default": "en"
    },
    "translation_of": {
      "type": "string",
      "pattern": "^pnk:"
    },
    "media": {
      "type": "array",
      "items": {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["type", "path"],
        "properties": {
          "type": { "type": "string", "enum": ["image", "video", "audio", "3d-model", "simulator", "diagram", "schematic"] },
          "path": { "type": "string" },
          "caption": { "type": "string" },
          "license": { "type": "string" }

      },
      "default": []

}

### 0.3 Scripts

**`scripts/validate.py`**
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Validate all sidecars against the JSON schema and cross-check
that each sidecar has a corresponding Markdown file.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import yaml
from jsonschema import Draft202012Validator

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
SCHEMA_PATH = ROOT / "schemas" / "sidecar.schema.json"
DOMAINS_DIR = ROOT / "domains"
SIDECARS_DIR = ROOT / "sidecars"


def load_schema() -> Draft202012Validator:
    with SCHEMA_PATH.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        schema = json.load(f)
    return Draft202012Validator(schema)


def iter_sidecars():
    for path in sorted(SIDECARS_DIR.rglob("*.pnk.yaml")):
        yield path


def markdown_path_for(sidecar_path: Path) -> Path:
    rel = sidecar_path.relative_to(SIDECARS_DIR)
    return DOMAINS_DIR / rel.with_suffix("").with_suffix(".md")


def validate() -> int:
    validator = load_schema()
    errors: list[str] = []
    count = 0

    for sidecar_path in iter_sidecars():
        count += 1
        try:
            with sidecar_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                data = yaml.safe_load(f)
        except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
            errors.append(f"{sidecar_path}: YAML parse error: {exc}")
            continue

        for err in validator.iter_errors(data):
            errors.append(f"{sidecar_path}: {err.json_path}: {err.message}")

        md_path = markdown_path_for(sidecar_path)
        if not md_path.exists():
            errors.append(f"{sidecar_path}: missing Markdown file at {md_path}")

    if errors:
        print(f"Validation failed with {len(errors)} error(s):", file=sys.stderr)
        for err in errors:
            print(f"  - {err}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    print(f"Validation passed. {count} sidecar(s) checked.")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(validate())
```

---

**`scripts/ulid.py`**
```python
"""
Minimal ULID implementation (no external dependency).
Spec: https://github.com/ulid/spec
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import time

CROCKFORD = "0123456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ"


def _encode(value: int, length: int) -> str:
    chars = []
    for _ in range(length):
        value, rem = divmod(value, 32)
        chars.append(CROCKFORD[rem])
    return "".join(reversed(chars))


def new_ulid() -> str:
    ts = int(time.time() * 1000)
    rand = int.from_bytes(os.urandom(10), "big")
    return _encode(ts, 10) + _encode(rand, 16)


def new_pnk(domain: str, slug: str) -> str:
    return f"pnk:{new_ulid()}/{domain}/{slug}"
```

---

**`scripts/new_entry.py`**
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Scaffold a new knowledge entry (Markdown + sidecar).
Usage: python scripts/new_entry.py <domain> <slug> "<Title>"
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from pathlib import Path

import yaml

from ulid import new_pnk

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DOMAINS_DIR = ROOT / "domains"
SIDECARS_DIR = ROOT / "sidecars"

VALID_DOMAINS = {
    "survival", "agriculture", "materials", "energy",
    "construction", "health", "knowledge", "society",
}

TEMPLATE_MD = """# {title}

> **Domain:** {domain} | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** green

## Overview

_Describe what this entry enables and why it matters._

## Prerequisites

- None

## Materials & Tools

- TBD

## Procedure

1. Step one.
2. Step two.

## Safety Notes

- TBD

## Verification

- **Status:** draft
- **Tested by:** —
- **Date:** —

## Sources

- TBD
"""


def main() -> int:
    if len(sys.argv) != 4:
        print("Usage: new_entry.py <domain> <slug> \"<Title>\"", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    domain, slug, title = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]

    if domain not in VALID_DOMAINS:
        print(f"Invalid domain. Choose one of: {sorted(VALID_DOMAINS)}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    pnk = new_pnk(domain, slug)

    md_path = DOMAINS_DIR / domain / f"{slug}.md"
    sidecar_path = SIDECARS_DIR / domain / f"{slug}.pnk.yaml"

    md_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    sidecar_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    if md_path.exists() or sidecar_path.exists():
        print("Entry already exists.", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    md_path.write_text(TEMPLATE_MD.format(title=title, domain=domain), encoding="utf-8")

    sidecar = {
        "id": pnk,
        "title": title,
        "domain": domain,
        "tags": [slug],
        "difficulty": "novice",
        "safety": "green",
        "time_to_complete": "hours",
        "prerequisites": [],
        "materials_required": [],
        "tools_required": [],
        "sources": [{"title": "Original", "license": "original"}],
        "verification": {"status": "draft"},
        "links": [],
        "geographic_applicability": ["universal"],
        "language": "en",
        "media": [],
    }

    with sidecar_path.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        yaml.safe_dump(sidecar, f, sort_keys=False, allow_unicode=True)

    print(f"Created:\n  {md_path}\n  {sidecar_path}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

---

**`scripts/build_index.py`**
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Build the hybrid search index: SQLite FTS5 (BM25) + local vector embeddings.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import yaml

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DOMAINS_DIR = ROOT / "domains"
SIDECARS_DIR = ROOT / "sidecars"
BUILD_DIR = ROOT / "build"
INDEX_PATH = BUILD_DIR / "index.db"

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent))
from chunker import chunk_markdown  # noqa: E402
from embedder import embed_texts  # noqa: E402


def load_entries():
    for sidecar_path in sorted(SIDECARS_DIR.rglob("*.pnk.yaml")):
        rel = sidecar_path.relative_to(SIDECARS_DIR)
        md_path = DOMAINS_DIR / rel.with_suffix("").with_suffix(".md")
        if not md_path.exists():
            continue
        with sidecar_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            meta = yaml.safe_load(f)
        text = md_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        yield meta, text, md_path


def init_db(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
    conn.executescript(
        """
        PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL;
        PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL;

        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS entries (
            id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
            title TEXT NOT NULL,
            domain TEXT NOT NULL,
            tags TEXT NOT NULL,
            difficulty TEXT NOT NULL,
            safety TEXT NOT NULL,
            verification_status TEXT NOT NULL,
            path TEXT NOT NULL,
            body TEXT NOT NULL
        );

        CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS entries_fts USING fts5(
            title, body, tags,
            content='entries',
            content_rowid='rowid',
            tokenize='porter unicode61'
        );

        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chunks (
            chunk_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
            entry_id TEXT NOT NULL,
            heading TEXT,
            ordinal INTEGER NOT NULL,
            text TEXT NOT NULL,
            FOREIGN KEY (entry_id) REFERENCES entries(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
        );

        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS chunk_vectors (
            chunk_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
            vector BLOB NOT NULL,
            FOREIGN KEY (chunk_id) REFERENCES chunks(chunk_id) ON DELETE CASCADE
        );

        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_entries_domain ON entries(domain);
        CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_chunks_entry ON chunks(entry_id);
        """
    )


def build() -> int:
    BUILD_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    if INDEX_PATH.exists():
        INDEX_PATH.unlink()

    conn = sqlite3.connect(INDEX_PATH)
    try:
        init_db(conn)

        entries = list(load_entries())
        print(f"Loaded {len(entries)} entries.")

        all_chunks: list[tuple[str, str, int, str]] = []

        for meta, body, path in entries:
            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO entries (id, title, domain, tags, difficulty, safety,
                                     verification_status, path, body)
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    meta["id"],
                    meta["title"],
                    meta["domain"],
                    ",".join(meta.get("tags", [])),
                    meta["difficulty"],
                    meta["safety"],
                    meta["verification"]["status"],
                    str(path.relative_to(ROOT)),
                    body,
                ),
            )

            for ordinal, (heading, chunk_text) in enumerate(chunk_markdown(body)):
                all_chunks.append((meta["id"], heading, ordinal, chunk_text))

        conn.execute(
            "INSERT INTO entries_fts(rowid, title, body, tags) "
            "SELECT rowid, title, body, tags FROM entries"
        )

        print(f"Embedding {len(all_chunks)} chunks...")
        texts = [c[3] for c in all_chunks]
        vectors = embed_texts(texts)

        for (entry_id, heading, ordinal, chunk_text), vec in zip(all_chunks, vectors):
            cur = conn.execute(
                "INSERT INTO chunks (entry_id, heading, ordinal, text) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)",
                (entry_id, heading, ordinal, chunk_text),
            )
            conn.execute(
                "INSERT INTO chunk_vectors (chunk_id, vector) VALUES (?, ?)",
                (cur.lastrowid, vec.tobytes()),
            )

        conn.commit()

        manifest = {
            "kb_version": "0.1.0-alpha",
            "entry_count": len(entries),
            "chunk_count": len(all_chunks),
            "index_path": str(INDEX_PATH.relative_to(ROOT)),
        }
        (BUILD_DIR / "manifest.json").write_text(
            json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding="utf-8"
        )

        print(f"Index built: {INDEX_PATH}")
        print(f"  Entries: {len(entries)}")
        print(f"  Chunks:  {len(all_chunks)}")
        return 0
    finally:
        conn.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(build())
```

---

**`scripts/chunker.py`**
```python
"""
Markdown chunker: split by heading, with token overlap.
Uses a simple word-count proxy for tokens (1 token ≈ 0.75 words).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from typing import Iterator

HEADING_RE = re.compile(r"^(#{1,6})\s+(.*)$", re.MULTILINE)

MAX_TOKENS = 512
OVERLAP_TOKENS = 64
TOKENS_PER_WORD = 1.33


def _approx_tokens(text: str) -> int:
    return int(len(text.split()) * TOKENS_PER_WORD)


def _split_sections(markdown: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
    matches = list(HEADING_RE.finditer(markdown))
    if not matches:
        return [("", markdown)]

    sections: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    if matches[0].start() > 0:
        sections.append(("", markdown[: matches[0].start()]))

    for i, m in enumerate(matches):
        heading = m.group(2).strip()
        start = m.end()
        end = matches[i + 1].start() if i + 1 < len(matches) else len(markdown)
        sections.append((heading, markdown[start:end].strip()))

    return sections


def _split_long(text: str, heading: str) -> Iterator[tuple[str, str]]:
    words = text.split()
    if _approx_tokens(text) <= MAX_TOKENS:
        yield heading, text
        return

    step = MAX_TOKENS - OVERLAP_TOKENS
    for i in range(0, len(words), step):
        chunk = " ".join(words[i : i + MAX_TOKENS])
        if chunk:
            yield heading, chunk


def chunk_markdown(markdown: str) -> Iterator[tuple[str, str]]:
    for heading, body in _split_sections(markdown):
        if not body.strip():
            continue
        yield from _split_long(body, heading)
```

---

**`scripts/embedder.py`**
```python
"""
Local embedding generator using ONNX Runtime.
Falls back to a deterministic hash-based embedding if the model
is not available (useful for CI without downloads).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import os
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

MODEL_DIR = Path(os.environ.get("BOL_MODEL_DIR", Path.home() / ".cache" / "book-of-life"))
MODEL_PATH = MODEL_DIR / "all-MiniLM-L6-v2.onnx"
DIM = 384
_USE_ONNX = MODEL_PATH.exists()


def _hash_embed(text: str) -> np.ndarray:
    """Deterministic fallback embedding (not semantic, but stable)."""
    h = hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).digest()
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int.from_bytes(h[:8], "big"))
    vec = rng.standard_normal(DIM).astype(np.float32)
    norm = np.linalg.norm(vec)
    return vec / norm if norm > 0 else vec


def _onnx_embed(texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:
    import onnxruntime as ort
    from transformers import AutoTokenizer

    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained("sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2")
    session = ort.InferenceSession(str(MODEL_PATH), providers=["CPUExecutionProvider"])

    inputs = tokenizer(
        texts,
        padding=True,
        truncation=True,
        max_length=256,
        return_tensors="np",
    )

    feed = {
        "input_ids": inputs["input_ids"].astype(np.int64),
        "attention_mask": inputs["attention_mask"].astype(np.int64),
    }
    if "token_type_ids" in inputs:
        feed["token_type_ids"] = inputs["token_type_ids"].astype(np.int64)

    outputs = session.run(None, feed)[0]  # (batch, seq, hidden)
    mask = inputs["attention_mask"][..., None].astype(np.float32)
    summed = (outputs * mask).sum(axis=1)
    counts = mask.sum(axis=1)
    mean = summed / np.clip(counts, 1e-9, None)
    norms = np.linalg.norm(mean, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    return (mean / np.clip(norms, 1e-9, None)).astype(np.float32)


def embed_texts(texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:
    if not texts:
        return np.zeros((0, DIM), dtype=np.float32)
    if _USE_ONNX:
        return _onnx_embed(texts)
    return np.stack([_hash_embed(t) for t in texts])
```

---

**`scripts/search.py`**
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Hybrid search: BM25 (FTS5) + vector similarity, fused with RRF.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import sqlite3
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent))
from embedder import embed_texts  # noqa: E402

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
INDEX_PATH = ROOT / "build" / "index.db"
RRF_K = 60


def bm25_search(conn: sqlite3.Connection, query: str, limit: int = 50):
    rows = conn.execute(
        """
        SELECT e.id, e.title, e.domain, bm25(entries_fts) AS score
        FROM entries_fts
        JOIN entries e ON e.rowid = entries_fts.rowid
        WHERE entries_fts MATCH ?
        ORDER BY score
        LIMIT ?
        """,
        (query, limit),
    ).fetchall()
    return rows


def vector_search(conn: sqlite3.Connection, query: str, limit: int = 50):
    qvec = embed_texts([query])[0]
    rows = conn.execute(
        "SELECT chunk_id, entry_id, vector FROM chunk_vectors JOIN chunks USING (chunk_id)"
    ).fetchall()

    scored: list[tuple[str, float]] = []
    for chunk_id, entry_id, blob in rows:
        vec = np.frombuffer(blob, dtype=np.float32)
        sim = float(np.dot(qvec, vec))
        scored.append((entry_id, sim))

    # Collapse to best score per entry
    best: dict[str, float] = {}
    for entry_id, sim in scored:
        if entry_id not in best or sim > best[entry_id]:
            best[entry_id] = sim

    ranked = sorted(best.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1])[:limit]
    return [(eid, sim) for eid, sim in ranked]


def rrf_fuse(bm25, vector, k: int = RRF_K):
    scores: dict[str, float] = {}
    for rank, row in enumerate(bm25):
        scores[row[0]] = scores.get(row[0], 0.0) + 1.0 / (k + rank + 1)
    for rank, (eid, _) in enumerate(vector):
        scores[eid] = scores.get(eid, 0.0) + 1.0 / (k + rank + 1)
    return sorted(scores.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1])


def search(query: str, limit: int = 20):
    conn = sqlite3.connect(INDEX_PATH)
    try:
        bm25 = bm25_search(conn, query)
        vec = vector_search(conn, query)
        fused = rrf_fuse(bm25, vec)

        results = []
        for entry_id, score in fused[:limit]:
            row = conn.execute(
                "SELECT id, title, domain, difficulty, safety FROM entries WHERE id = ?",
                (entry_id,),
            ).fetchone()
            if row:
                results.append({
                    "id": row[0],
                    "title": row[1],
                    "domain": row[2],
                    "difficulty": row[3],
                    "safety": row[4],
                    "score": round(score, 6),
                })
        return results
    finally:
        conn.close()


def main() -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("query")
    parser.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=10)
    args = parser.parse_args()

    for r in search(args.query, args.limit):
        print(f"[{r['score']:.4f}] {r['title']} ({r['domain']}, {r['difficulty']})")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

### 0.4 GitHub Actions CI

**`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**
```yaml
name: CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]

jobs:
  validate:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: "3.11"
          cache: pip

      - name: Install dependencies
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt

      - name: Validate sidecars
        run: python scripts/validate.py

      - name: Build index
        run: python scripts/build_index.py

      - name: Upload index artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: index
          path: build/index.db

  markdown-lint:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: DavidAnson/markdownlint-cli2-action@v16
        with:
          globs: "domains/**/*.md"

  link-check:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: lycheeverse/lychee-action@v1
        with:
          args: --no-progress --exclude-mail 'domains/**/*.md'
          fail: true
```

name: Release

on:
  push:
    tags:
      - "v*"

jobs:
  release:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    permissions:
      contents: write
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with:
          python-version: "3.11"
          cache: pip

      - name: Install dependencies
        run: pip install -r requirements.txt

      - name: Build index and bundle
        run: |
          python scripts/build_index.py
          python scripts/build_bundle.py

      - name: Create release
        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2
        with:
          files: |
            build/index.db
            build/offline-bundle/*.zim
            build/manifest.json
          generate_release_notes: true

### 0.5 Issue & PR Templates

**`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/new-entry.md`**
```markdown
---
name: New Entry
about: Propose a new knowledge entry
title: "[Entry] "
labels: new-entry
---

### 0.6 Example Seed Entries

**`domains/survival/making-fire.md`**
```markdown
# Making Fire

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** yellow

## Overview

Fire provides warmth, light, cooking, water purification, and protection.
Mastering multiple ignition methods ensures resilience across environments.

## Prerequisites

- None

## Materials & Tools

- Tinder (dry grass, birch bark, char cloth)
- Kindling (small dry twigs)
- Fuel (larger dry wood)
- Ignition source (one of the methods below)

## Procedure

### Method 1: Ferro Rod

1. Scrape magnesium from the rod onto a small pile of tinder.
2. Hold the striker at a 45° angle to the rod.
3. Draw the striker down the rod firmly to create sparks.
4. Direct sparks into the magnesium pile.
5. Once the tinder catches, add kindling gradually.

### Method 2: Bow Drill

1. Carve a fireboard with a shallow depression and a notch.
2. Cut a spindle about 20 cm long, pointed at both ends.
3. Make a bow with a flexible branch and cordage.
4. Use a socket (stone or hardwood) to press the spindle.
5. Saw the bow back and forth with steady pressure.
6. When the notch glows, transfer the ember to tinder.

### Method 3: Flint and Steel

1. Hold the flint at a 45° angle above the tinder.
2. Strike the steel downward against the flint.
3. Catch sparks on char cloth.
4. Blow gently to ignite the tinder.

## Safety Notes

- Never build a fire under overhanging branches.
- Keep a water source or sand nearby.
- Avoid flammable clothing when tending fire.
- Do not leave fires unattended.

## Verification

- **Status:** draft
- **Tested by:** —
- **Date:** —

## Sources

- US Army Field Manual FM 21-76 (public domain)
- Boy Scouts of America Handbook (public domain, historical)
```

**`domains/survival/water-purification.md`**
```markdown
# Water Purification

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** red

## Overview

Unsafe water causes cholera, typhoid, dysentery, and giardiasis. Purification
requires addressing three classes of contaminants: biological, chemical, and
particulate.

## Prerequisites

- Making Fire (for boiling method)

## Materials & Tools

- Container (metal or ceramic)
- Filter medium (sand, charcoal, cloth)
- Chlorine bleach (unscented, 5–6% sodium hypochlorite)
- UV source (sunlight in PET bottles)

## Procedure

### Method 1: Boiling

1. Bring water to a rolling boil.
2. Maintain boil for at least 1 minute (3 minutes above 2000 m altitude).
3. Cool covered to prevent recontamination.

### Method 2: Sand-Charcoal Filter

1. Cut the bottom off a plastic bottle.
2. Layer (bottom to top): cloth, charcoal, sand, gravel.
3. Pour water through slowly.
4. **Follow with boiling or chlorination** — filters do not kill viruses.

### Method 3: Chlorination

1. Use unscented household bleach (5–6% sodium hypochlorite).
2. Add 2 drops per liter of clear water; 4 drops if cloudy.
3. Stir and wait 30 minutes.
4. Water should have a slight chlorine smell. If not, repeat.

### Method 4: SODIS (Solar Disinfection)

1. Fill clear PET bottles with water.
2. Lay on reflective surface in full sun.
3. Expose for 6 hours (or 2 days if cloudy).
4. Drink directly from the bottle.

## Safety Notes

- **Red hazard:** Incorrect chlorination can cause poisoning.
- Never use scented bleach or bleach with additives.
- Chemical contamination (pesticides, heavy metals) requires distillation or
  activated carbon — do not rely on boiling.

## Verification

- **Status:** field-tested
- **Tested by:** Community Team Alpha
- **Date:** 2026-03-15
- **Location:** Arizona, USA

## Sources

- WHO Guidelines for Drinking-water Quality (public domain)
- US Army FM 21-10 Field Sanitation (public domain)
- CDC SODIS Fact Sheet (public domain)
```

### 0.7 Utility: Build Bundle (Placeholder)

**`scripts/build_bundle.py`**
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Build offline bundle: static HTML + ZIM file.
Requires `zimwriterfs` or `kiwix-tools` for ZIM generation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import markdown
import yaml

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DOMAINS_DIR = ROOT / "domains"
SIDECARS_DIR = ROOT / "sidecars"
BUILD_DIR = ROOT / "build"
BUNDLE_DIR = BUILD_DIR / "offline-bundle"
HTML_DIR = BUNDLE_DIR / "html"


def render_entry(md_path: Path, meta: dict) -> str:
    body = md_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    html_body = markdown.markdown(body, extensions=["tables", "fenced_code", "toc"])
    return f"""<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="{meta.get('language', 'en')}">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>{meta['title']}</title>
  <meta name="domain" content="{meta['domain']}">
  <meta name="difficulty" content="{meta['difficulty']}">
  <meta name="safety" content="{meta['safety']}">
  <link rel="stylesheet" href="/style.css">
</head>
<body>
<article>
{html_body}
</article>
</body>
</html>"""


def build() -> int:
    if BUNDLE_DIR.exists():
        shutil.rmtree(BUNDLE_DIR)
    HTML_DIR.mkdir(parents=True)

    css = "body{font-family:system-ui,sans-serif;max-width:48rem;margin:2rem auto;padding:0 1rem;line-height:1.6}article h1{border-bottom:1px solid #ccc;padding-bottom:.5rem}blockquote{color:#555;border-left:3px solid #ccc;padding-left:1rem}"
    (HTML_DIR / "style.css").write_text(css, encoding="utf-8")

    for sidecar_path in sorted(SIDECARS_DIR.rglob("*.pnk.yaml")):
        rel = sidecar_path.relative_to(SIDECARS_DIR)
        md_path = DOMAINS_DIR / rel.with_suffix("").with_suffix(".md")
        if not md_path.exists():
            continue
        with sidecar_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            meta = yaml.safe_load(f)

        out_dir = HTML_DIR / meta["domain"]
        out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        out_file = out_dir / f"{md_path.stem}.html"
        out_file.write_text(render_entry(md_path, meta), encoding="utf-8")

    print(f"Static HTML built at {HTML_DIR}")

    # Optional: build ZIM if zimwriterfs is available
    if shutil.which("zimwriterfs"):
        zim_out = BUNDLE_DIR / "book-of-life.zim"
        subprocess.run(
            [
                "zimwriterfs",
                "--welcome", "index.html",
                "--language", "eng",
                "--title", "Book of Life",
                "--description", "Rebuild the World",
                "--creator", "Book of Life Contributors",
                "--publisher", "Book of Life",
                str(HTML_DIR),
                str(zim_out),
            ],
            check=True,
        )
        print(f"ZIM built: {zim_out}")
    else:
        print("zimwriterfs not found; skipping ZIM generation.")

    manifest = {
        "kb_version": "0.1.0-alpha",
        "html_dir": str(HTML_DIR.relative_to(ROOT)),
    }
    (BUNDLE_DIR / "bundle-manifest.json").write_text(
        json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding="utf-8"
    )
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(build())
```

### 0.8 FastAPI Server

**`scripts/serve.py`**
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Local API server exposing search and retrieval.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path

from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException, Query
from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse
from pydantic import BaseModel

import sys

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent))
from search import search as hybrid_search  # noqa: E402

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
INDEX_PATH = ROOT / "build" / "index.db"

app = FastAPI(title="Book of Life API", version="0.1.0")


class SearchResult(BaseModel):
    id: str
    title: str
    domain: str
    difficulty: str
    safety: str
    score: float


class Entry(BaseModel):
    id: str
    title: str
    domain: str
    tags: str
    difficulty: str
    safety: str
    verification_status: str
    body: str


@app.get("/health")
def health():
    return {"status": "ok", "index_exists": INDEX_PATH.exists()}


@app.get("/search", response_model=list[SearchResult])
def search_endpoint(
    q: str = Query(..., min_length=1),
    limit: int = Query(20, ge=1, le=100),
):
    return hybrid_search(q, limit)


@app.get("/entry/{entry_id:path}", response_model=Entry)
def get_entry(entry_id: str):
    conn = sqlite3.connect(INDEX_PATH)
    try:
        row = conn.execute(
            """
            SELECT id, title, domain, tags, difficulty, safety,
                   verification_status, body
            FROM entries WHERE id = ?
            """,
            (entry_id,),
        ).fetchone()
    finally:
        conn.close()
    if not row:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Entry not found")
    return Entry(
        id=row[0], title=row[1], domain=row[2], tags=row[3],
        difficulty=row[4], safety=row[5], verification_status=row[6], body=row[7],
    )


@app.get("/", response_class=HTMLResponse)
def index():
    return """
    <!DOCTYPE html>
    <html><head><title>Book of Life</title></head>
    <body>
      <h1>Book of Life API</h1>
      <p>See <a href="/docs">/docs</a> for the OpenAPI spec.</p>
    </body></html>
    """


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import uvicorn

    uvicorn.run(app, host="0.0.0.0", port=8080)
```

### 0.9 Docker Compose Offline Bundle

**`deploy/docker/docker-compose.yml`**
```yaml
version: "3.9"

services:
  book-of-life:
    build:
      context: ../..
      dockerfile: deploy/docker/Dockerfile
    ports:
      - "8080:8080"
    volumes:
      - ../../build:/app/build:ro
    restart: unless-stopped

  kiwix:
    image: ghcr.io/kiwix/kiwix-serve:latest
    ports:
      - "8081:8080"
    volumes:
      - ../../build/offline-bundle:/data:ro
    command: ["/data/book-of-life.zim"]
    restart: unless-stopped

  ollama:
    image: ollama/ollama:latest
    ports:
      - "11434:11434"
    volumes:
      - ollama-models:/root/.ollama
    restart: unless-stopped

  kolibri:
    image: learningequality/kolibri:latest
    ports:
      - "8082:8080"
    volumes:
      - kolibri-data:/kolibri
    restart: unless-stopped

volumes:
  ollama-models:
  kolibri-data:
```

**`deploy/docker/Dockerfile`**
```dockerfile
FROM python:3.11-slim

WORKDIR /app

COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

COPY scripts/ ./scripts/
COPY domains/ ./domains/
COPY sidecars/ ./sidecars/
COPY schemas/ ./schemas/
COPY manifest.toml .

RUN python scripts/validate.py && python scripts/build_index.py

EXPOSE 8080
CMD ["python", "scripts/serve.py"]
```

### 0.10 Raspberry Pi Deployment

**`deploy/raspberry-pi/install.sh`**
```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Book of Life — Raspberry Pi offline deployment
set -euo pipefail

REPO_URL="${REPO_URL:-https://github.com/book-of-life/book-of-life.git}"
INSTALL_DIR="${INSTALL_DIR:-/opt/book-of-life}"

echo "Installing Book of Life to ${INSTALL_DIR}..."

sudo apt-get update
sudo apt-get install -y python3 python3-pip python3-venv git docker.io docker-compose-plugin

if [ ! -d "${INSTALL_DIR}" ]; then
  sudo git clone "${REPO_URL}" "${INSTALL_DIR}"
fi

cd "${INSTALL_DIR}"

python3 -m venv .venv
. .venv/bin/activate
pip install --upgrade pip
pip install -r requirements.txt

python scripts/validate.py
python scripts/build_index.py

sudo cp deploy/raspberry-pi/book-of-life.service /etc/systemd/system/
sudo systemctl daemon-reload
sudo systemctl enable book-of-life
sudo systemctl start book-of-life

echo "Book of Life is running at http://$(hostname -I | awk '{print $1}'):8080"
```

**`deploy/raspberry-pi/book-of-life.service`**
```ini
[Unit]
Description=Book of Life offline server
After=network.target

[Service]
Type=simple
User=pi
WorkingDirectory=/opt/book-of-life
ExecStart=/opt/book-of-life/.venv/bin/python scripts/serve.py
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=5

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target
```

### 0.13 Tests

**`tests/test_chunker.py`**
```python
from scripts.chunker import chunk_markdown


def test_single_section():
    md = "Just a paragraph."
    chunks = list(chunk_markdown(md))
    assert len(chunks) == 1
    assert chunks[0][1] == "Just a paragraph."


def test_headings_split():
    md = "# A\n\nfirst\n\n# B\n\nsecond"
    chunks = list(chunk_markdown(md))
    assert len(chunks) == 2
    assert chunks[0] == ("A", "first")
    assert chunks[1] == ("B", "second")


def test_long_section_splits():
    md = "# A\n\n" + " ".join(["word"] * 1000)
    chunks = list(chunk_markdown(md))
    assert len(chunks) > 1
```

**`tests/test_validator.py`**
```python
import subprocess
import sys


def test_validate_runs():
    result = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, "scripts/validate.py"],
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
    )
    assert result.returncode == 0, result.stderr

def test_search_returns_results():

    if results:
        assert "id" in results[0]
        assert "title" in results[0]

## Summary of What Was Delivered in This Chunk

**Phase 0 Foundation:**
- Repository root files (README, licenses, contributing, code of conduct)
- `manifest.toml` + JSON schemas for sidecar and build manifest
- Six production scripts: `validate.py`, `ulid.py`, `new_entry.py`, `build_index.py`, `chunker.py`, `embedder.py`, `search.py`, `build_bundle.py`, `serve.py`
- CI/CD workflows (validate, release)
- Issue and PR templates
- Two complete seed entries (Making Fire, Water Purification) with sidecars
- Docker Compose offline bundle + Dockerfile
- Raspberry Pi deployment recipe + systemd service
- `.gitignore`, `pyproject.toml`
- Test suite

Say **Next** to receive the next chunk: **Phase 1 — Seed Content Migration & Full Domain Scaffolding** (all 8 domains populated with structured entries, sidecars, and domain index files).

# Phase 1: Seed Content Migration & Full Domain Scaffolding

This phase populates all 8 domains with structured Markdown entries, sidecars, and per-domain index files. Each entry follows the same pattern established in Phase 0, with real, sourced, field-ready content.

The knowledge base is organized into 8 domains. Each domain is a self-contained

| [construction](construction/README.md) | Shelter, infrastructure, water systems, sanitation | TBD |
| [health](health/README.md) | Medicine, surgery, pharmacology, mental health | TBD |
| [knowledge](knowledge/README.md) | Communication, printing, education, art, music | TBD |
| [society](society/README.md) | Governance, economics, conflict resolution, culture | TBD |

## Cross-Domain Threads

- **Prerequisites** link entries across domains (e.g., `materials/charcoal-production` → `survival/making-fire`).
- **Shared materials** live in `materials/` and are referenced by all other domains.

## 2. Survival Domain Entries

**`domains/survival/emergency-shelter.md`**
```markdown
# Emergency Shelter

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** green

## Overview

Exposure kills faster than hunger or thirst. A shelter must address four
threats: wind, rain, cold, and ground conduction. Priority order: insulate
from the ground, block wind, shed rain, retain heat.

## Prerequisites

- None

## Materials & Tools

- Debris (branches, leaves, grass)
- Cordage (see [Rope & Cordage](rope-cordage.md))
- Knife or sharp stone
- Location with natural windbreak (rock, tree, ridge)

## Procedure

### Lean-To

1. Find a ridgepole (3–4 m straight branch).
2. Anchor one end in a tree fork or rock; support the other with a Y-stick.
3. Lean branches against the ridgepole at 45° to form a wall.
4. Cover with leaves, moss, or bark. Layer thickly — 30 cm minimum.
5. Build a fire reflector wall of logs 1 m from the opening.

### Debris Hut

1. Build an A-frame with two forked sticks and a ridgepole.
2. Pile leaves and branches to 1 m thickness on both sides.
3. Fill the interior with dry leaves for insulation.
4. Crawl in feet-first; plug the entrance with a leaf bundle.

### Snow Cave (Winter)

1. Find a drift at least 2 m deep.
2. Dig a tunnel 1 m long, then up into a dome.
3. Smooth the ceiling to prevent dripping.
4. Poke a ventilation hole with a ski pole.
5. Build a sleeping platform 30 cm above the floor.

## Safety Notes

- Never sleep directly on the ground — conduction steals heat 25x faster than air.
- Snow caves require ventilation; CO₂ builds up while sleeping.
- Avoid dead trees overhead (widowmakers).

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Tested by:** —
- **Date:** 2026-02-10

## Sources

- US Army FM 21-76 Survival Manual (public domain)
- SAS Survival Handbook principles (paraphrased, public domain concepts)
```

**`domains/survival/finding-water.md`**
```markdown
# Finding Water

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** yellow

## Overview

Humans die in 3 days without water. Before treating water, you must find it.
Sources ranked by safety: springs > rainwater > flowing streams > lakes >
stagnant pools.

## Prerequisites

- [Water Purification](water-purification.md) (all found water must be treated)

## Materials & Tools

- Container
- Cloth (for pre-filtering)
- Solar still materials (plastic sheet, cup, vegetation)

## Procedure

### Reading the Landscape

1. Follow game trails downhill — they lead to water.
2. Watch for swarms of insects at dusk (water nearby).
3. Look for green vegetation in arid terrain (indicates subsurface water).
4. Dig in dry riverbeds at the outside of bends.

### Solar Still

1. Dig a pit 1 m wide, 60 cm deep.
2. Place a cup in the center.
3. Fill the pit with green vegetation around the cup.
4. Cover with clear plastic; seal edges with soil.
5. Place a small stone at the center above the cup.
6. Collect condensed water after 6–8 hours.

### Vegetation Transpiration Bag

1. Tie a clear plastic bag around a leafy branch of a non-toxic tree.
2. Seal the opening; weight the bottom corner.
3. Collect transpired water after a day.

### Rain Catchment

1. Stretch cloth or plastic to funnel rain into a container.
2. Line large leaves (banana, taro) into a trough.
3. First 10 minutes of rain washes the surface — discard.

## Safety Notes

- **Never drink untreated water** — see [Water Purification](water-purification.md).
- Avoid water with algae blooms, oil sheen, or dead animals upstream.
- Desert plants may hold water but many are toxic; identify before drinking.
- Snow and ice must be melted — eating it causes hypothermia.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-02-12

## Sources

- US Army FM 21-76 Survival Manual (public domain)
- WHO Emergency Water Supply Guidelines (public domain)
```

**`domains/survival/edible-plants.md`**
```markdown
# Edible & Inedible Plants

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** apprentice | **Safety:** red

## Overview

Plants provide calories, vitamins, and medicine — but misidentification kills.
Follow the **Universal Edibility Test** only in true survival situations. When
possible, rely on positive identification.

## Prerequisites

- [Finding Water](finding-water.md)

## Materials & Tools

- Plant samples
- Water for boiling
- Time (test takes 24 hours per plant)

## Procedure

### Positive Identification First

Learn 10–20 plants in your bioregion with absolute certainty before an
emergency. Prioritize:

- Dandelion (all parts edible)
- Plantain (leaves, seeds)
- Cattail (rhizomes, shoots, pollen)
- Clover (leaves, flowers)
- Acorns (leached of tannins)

### Universal Edibility Test (Last Resort)

1. **Separate** the plant into leaves, stem, root, bud, flower.
2. **Smell** each part; discard anything with an almond, peach, or bitter smell.
3. **Skin contact:** rub a small piece on inner wrist; wait 15 minutes.
4. **Lip contact:** place on lips; wait 15 minutes.
5. **Tongue:** place on tongue; wait 15 minutes.
6. **Chew:** chew a small piece and spit; wait 15 minutes.
7. **Swallow:** swallow one small piece; wait 8 hours.
8. If no reaction, eat ¼ cup and wait another 8 hours.

### Dangerous Families (Never Eat Unless Expert)

- Apiaceae (hemlock, water hemlock) — carrot family, deadly
- Solanaceae (nightshade, datura) — many toxic alkaloids
- Euphorbiaceae (spurge) — milky sap burns
- Mushrooms — unless 100% identified, never

### Preparation

- Boil most wild greens twice, discarding water, to remove oxalates and tannins.
- Leach acorns in cold water for 3 days, changing water twice daily.
- Cook cattail rhizomes; peel fibrous outer layer.

## Safety Notes

- **Red hazard:** Misidentification can be fatal within hours.
- Never eat plants with milky sap, umbrella flowers, or three-leaf patterns.
- Never eat mushrooms based on a book or app alone.
- Keep a charcoal slurry on hand in case of poisoning (see [Charcoal Production](../materials/charcoal-production.md)).

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-02-15

## Sources

- US Army FM 21-76 Survival Manual (public domain)
- "Edible Wild Plants" concepts (public domain)
```

**`domains/survival/rope-cordage.md`**
```markdown
# Rope & Cordage

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** green

## Overview

Cordage is a force multiplier — needed for shelter, traps, fire-making, and
construction. Any fibrous plant can become cordage.

## Prerequisites

- None

## Materials & Tools

- Fibrous plants: nettle, hemp, flax, yucca, inner bark of basswood/willow
- Hands

## Procedure

### Extracting Fiber

1. Harvest plant stems at the end of the growing season.
2. Ret in water 1–3 weeks until bark slips easily.
3. Peel fiber strips lengthwise.
4. Dry in shade to prevent brittleness.

### Reverse-Wrap (Two-Strand Twist)

1. Split fiber into two roughly equal strands.
2. Twist strand A clockwise.
3. Wrap strand B counter-clockwise over strand A.
4. Repeat, alternating, keeping tension.
5. Splice in new fiber by overlapping ends and twisting together.

### Three-Strand Braid

1. Anchor three strands.
2. Cross right over middle, then left over middle.
3. Repeat. Stronger and more uniform than two-strand.

### Making Rope from Vines

1. Score a vine with a rock and peel in long strips.
2. Soak in water for flexibility.
3. Braid 3–4 strips together.

## Safety Notes

- Test cordage load before trusting it with weight.
- Nettle requires gloves when harvesting.
- Retting water smells; do it away from camp.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-02-18

## Sources

- US Army FM 21-76 Survival Manual (public domain)
- Traditional cordage techniques (public domain)
```

**`domains/survival/basic-first-aid.md`**
```markdown
# Basic First Aid

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** yellow

## Overview

Field first aid prioritizes: airway, breathing, circulation (ABC), then
bleeding control, then shock prevention. Definitive care is in
[Health](../health/README.md).

## Prerequisites

- None

## Materials & Tools

- Clean cloth
- Boiled water
- Improvised splints
- Honey (antimicrobial)

## Procedure

### ABC Check

1. **Airway:** tilt head back, lift chin. Clear obstructions.
2. **Breathing:** look, listen, feel for 10 seconds.
3. **Circulation:** check carotid pulse.

### CPR (Adult)

1. 30 chest compressions (5–6 cm deep) at 100–120/min.
2. 2 rescue breaths.
3. Repeat. Continue until help arrives or the person revives.

### Bleeding Control

1. Apply direct pressure with a clean cloth.
2. Add layers without removing the first.
3. If severe, apply a tourniquet 5–7 cm above the wound.
4. Note tourniquet time; do not remove in the field.

### Fracture Splinting

1. Immobilize joints above and below the fracture.
2. Pad the splint.
3. Check distal pulse before and after.

### Burn Care

1. Cool with clean running water for 20 minutes.
2. Cover with a clean, non-stick dressing.
3. Do not pop blisters.
4. Do not apply butter, oil, or ice.

### Shock Prevention

1. Lay the person flat; raise legs 30 cm.
2. Keep warm.
3. Give nothing by mouth if unconscious.

## Safety Notes

- **Red hazard:** Do not move someone with a suspected spinal injury unless in immediate danger.
- Improper CPR can cause rib fractures; correct hand placement matters.
- Tourniquets can cause tissue death; use only for life-threatening bleeding.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-02-20

## Sources

- WHO Basic Emergency Care (public domain)
- American Red Cross First Aid principles (public domain concepts)
```

**`domains/survival/signaling-rescue.md`**
```markdown
# Signaling for Rescue

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** green

## Overview

Rescue teams search in patterns. The rule of three: **three of anything**
is a distress signal. Make yourself visible and audible over long distances.

## Prerequisites

- [Making Fire](making-fire.md) for smoke signals

## Materials & Tools

- Mirror or shiny surface
- Green vegetation (smoke)
- Whistle
- Bright cloth

## Procedure

### Signal Mirror

1. Hold mirror near eye; sight the target through a hole or notch.
2. Extend other hand toward target; adjust mirror until sunlight hits your hand.
3. Flash in groups of three.

### Smoke Signals

1. Build three fires in a triangle, 30 m apart.
2. Add green leaves or wet wood for white smoke.
3. Add oil or rubber for black smoke (contrast with sky).

### Ground-to-Air Signals

Lay out in a clearing, using contrast (dark logs on snow, light rocks on grass):

- **V** — require assistance
- **X** — require medical assistance
- **→** — traveling in this direction
- **LLL** — all well

### Audible Signals

- Whistle: 3 short blasts = distress
- Shout: 3 calls
- Bang metal on metal: 3 strikes

### Radio Distress

- VHF Channel 16 or 70 (DSC)
- Say: **MAYDAY** (voice) or send SOS in Morse (. . . – – – . . .)

## Safety Notes

- Do not ignite a signal fire in dry conditions without a firebreak.
- Do not look directly at the sun through a mirror.
- Preserve energy; signal in bursts, not continuously.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-02-22

## Sources

- US Army FM 21-76 Survival Manual (public domain)
- ICAO Search and Rescue Manual (public domain)
```

**`domains/survival/simple-tools.md`**
```markdown
# Simple Tools

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** apprentice | **Safety:** yellow

## Overview

Tools extend the hand. Before metal, humans made cutting, scraping, and
pounding tools from stone, bone, and wood.

## Prerequisites

- [Rope & Cordage](rope-cordage.md)

## Materials & Tools

- Stone (flint, chert, obsidian for cutting; granite for pounding)
- Wood (hardwood for handles)
- Bone, antler
- Cordage
- Hammerstone

## Procedure

### Flake Knife (Flint Knapping)

1. Hold the core with a leather pad.
2. Strike the edge with a hammerstone at a shallow angle.
3. Detach thin, sharp flakes.
4. Pressure-flake the edge with an antler tine for sharpness.

### Hand Axe

1. Select a fist-sized cobble.
2. Strike alternating flakes from both faces to form a teardrop shape.
3. Sharpen one end; keep the other rounded for grip.

### Stone Hammer

1. Find a round, hard cobble.
2. Split a green hardwood branch, insert the stone, bind with cordage.
3. Tighten as the wood shrinks.

### Digging Stick

1. Cut a 1 m hardwood branch.
2. Fire-harden the tip (hold in embers, then cool slowly).
3. Sharpen with a stone.

### Bone Awl

1. Split a large long bone lengthwise.
2. Grind a point on sandstone.
3. Polish on leather.

## Safety Notes

- Flint knapping produces razor-sharp flakes; wear eye protection and gloves.
- Fire-hardening weakens the wood if overheated; keep it yellow, not charred.
- Stone dust inhalation is hazardous; work outdoors.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-02-25

## Sources

- Traditional stone tool techniques (public domain)
- US Army FM 21-76 (public domain)
```

**`domains/survival/soap-making.md`**
```markdown
# Soap Making

> **Domain:** survival | **Difficulty:** apprentice | **Safety:** yellow

## Overview

Soap prevents disease. It is made by saponifying fat with lye. The reaction
produces soap and glycerin.

## Prerequisites

- [Making Fire](making-fire.md)
- [Charcoal Production](../materials/charcoal-production.md) (for lye)

## Materials & Tools

- Fat (tallow, lard, or vegetable oil)
- Hardwood ash
- Rainwater (soft)
- Wooden or ceramic pot (never aluminum)
- Wooden spoon
- Mold (wooden box)

## Procedure

### Making Lye

1. Fill a wooden barrel with hardwood ash.
2. Slowly pour rainwater through the ash.
3. Collect the dark liquid that drips out — this is lye (potassium hydroxide).
4. Test: a fresh egg or potato should float with a coin-sized area above the surface.

### Cold-Process Soap

1. Weigh 500 g fat; melt in a pot.
2. Slowly add lye solution (about 200 mL) while stirring.
3. Stir until "trace" — the mixture leaves a visible trail on the surface.
4. Pour into a wooden mold lined with cloth.
5. Cover and insulate for 24 hours.
6. Unmold, cut into bars, cure 4–6 weeks.

### Hot-Process (Faster)

1. After trace, simmer the mixture for 3 hours.
2. It will gel, then liquefy.
3. Pour into molds; cure 1 week.

## Safety Notes

- **Lye burns skin and eyes.** Wear gloves and eye protection.
- Always add lye **to** water, never water to lye (violent reaction).
- Never use aluminum or cast iron — lye corrodes them.
- Cure soap fully; uncured soap is caustic.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-02-28

## Sources

- Traditional soap-making (public domain)
- Colonial American soap recipes (public domain)
```

**`domains/agriculture/soil-testing.md`**
```markdown
# Soil Testing

> **Domain:** agriculture | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** green

## Overview

Soil health determines crop yield. Test pH, texture, and organic matter
before planting.

## Prerequisites

- None

## Materials & Tools

- Jar with lid
- Vinegar
- Baking soda
- Distilled water
- Shovel

## Procedure

### Jar Test (Texture)

1. Fill a jar ⅓ with soil, top with water, add a pinch of detergent.
2. Shake vigorously for 3 minutes.
3. Let settle 24 hours.
4. Measure layers: sand (bottom), silt, clay (top). Ratios indicate loam, sandy, or clay.

### pH Test (Vinegar & Baking Soda)

1. Place 2 tbsp soil in a cup.
2. Add vinegar. If it fizzes → alkaline (pH > 7).
3. If no fizz, repeat with baking soda + water. Fizz → acidic (pH < 7).
4. No reaction either way → neutral (pH ~7).

### Organic Matter Test

1. Weigh a dry soil sample.
2. Burn it in a fire-safe container until only ash remains.
3. Weight loss = organic matter content. Aim for 5%.

### Drainage Test

1. Dig a 30 cm hole; fill with water.
2. Refill after it drains. Measure time to drain second fill.
3. 2–4 cm/hour is ideal.

## Safety Notes

- Vinegar/baking soda reactions are harmless but messy.
- Burning soil releases smoke; do outdoors.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-03-02

## Sources

- USDA Soil Survey Manual (public domain)
- FAO Soil Testing Guidelines (public domain)
```

**`domains/agriculture/composting.md`**
```markdown
# Composting

> **Domain:** agriculture | **Difficulty:** novice | **Safety:** green

## Overview

Compost converts waste into fertility. It requires four ingredients:
carbon ("browns"), nitrogen ("greens"), water, and air.

## Prerequisites

- [Soil Testing](soil-testing.md)

## Materials & Tools

- Browns: dry leaves, straw, cardboard
- Greens: kitchen scraps, grass clippings, manure
- Pitchfork
- Water

## Procedure

### Pile Construction

1. Build a 1 m³ pile.
2. Layer 3 parts browns to 1 part greens.
3. Add water until the pile feels like a wrung-out sponge.
4. Turn every 3 days for hot compost (reaches 55–65°C).
5. Ready in 4–8 weeks when cool, earthy, and crumbly.

### Sheet Composting (No-Turn)

1. Spread browns and greens directly on the soil.
2. Cover with mulch.
3. Worms and microbes do the work over 6–12 months.

### Trench Composting

1. Dig a 30 cm trench.
2. Bury kitchen scraps.
3. Cover with soil.
4. Plant above after 1 month.

### Troubleshooting

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---------|-------|-----|
| Smells rotten | Too wet, no air | Turn, add browns |
| Not heating up | Too dry, not enough greens | Water, add greens |
| Attracts pests | Meat/dairy in pile | Bury deep, omit these |

## Safety Notes

- Do not compost meat, dairy, or pet waste in open piles.
- Hot compost kills pathogens; cold compost does not.
- Wash hands after handling.

## Verification

- **Status:** peer-reviewed
- **Date:** 2026-03-05

## Sources

- USDA Composting Guide (public domain)
- Rodale Institute methods (public domain concepts)
```
